# Nectarine

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="nectarine-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="nectarine-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="nectarine-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Nectarine in the HMI library has 64 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn from 2 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury and nickel and others, in 64 results from 2 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the nectarine was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
</ul>
</section>


<a id="decision-summary"></a>
## Nectarine overview

<a id="hmi-ing-004"></a>
<a id="which-contamination-concerns-are-supported-for-this-ingredient-and-its-defined-forms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-005"></a>
<a id="which-sourcing-or-production-decisions-deserve-priority-on-the-available-evidence"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>
<a id="which-evidence-gaps-most-limit-those-decisions"></a>

Nectarine in the HMI library has 64 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn from 2 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in nectarine, juice from 194 results in 3 reporting regions, with a subgroup median of 2.53 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, GEMS/Food gave a subgroup median of 4.28 µg/kg from 220 results.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>



For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the nectarine came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as nectarine

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers nectarine, juice (64 results).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

<a id="heavy-metal-contamination-profile"></a>
## Heavy metal contamination profile of nectarine

<a id="hmi-ing-007"></a>
<a id="which-metals-and-chemical-species-have-been-investigated-in-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-008"></a>
<a id="what-concentrations-detection-frequencies-and-variability-were-observed-in-each-defined-sampling-frame"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-009"></a>
<a id="which-analyte-questions-are-unassessed-access-blocked-conflicting-or-searched-without-eligible-evidence"></a>

The table holds every extracted nectarine result: one row for each statistic a source reports for a form, group and metal, 64 rows in all, grouped by metal and form; percentiles, ranges and maxima for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Monitoring medians are HMI calculations from individual submissions; non-detects remain intervals between zero and the reporting limit and are never read as zero.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

0 rows can be rated against an HMI reference for the same form and basis, 2 rows have a direct same-study comparison, and the rest stay unrated with the reason recorded. 35 values from 1 source failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.<sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-nectarine" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-nectarine-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of nectarine, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

</div></details><p class="hmi-explorer-description" data-result-synopsis hidden><strong>First displayed analyte:</strong> <span data-result-synopsis-text>Lead: 7 findings from 1 source. Nectarine, juice; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤1.5</td><td>≤2</td><td>1.2–5 (bounded)</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤1.5</td><td>≤2</td><td>1.2–5 (bounded)</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>9 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>9</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for nectarine, juice on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for nectarine, juice on this basis in the completed nectarine ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>2</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for nectarine, juice on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for nectarine, juice on this basis in the completed nectarine ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:nectarine-90b67b07c9f5:1e9267ecf2fa</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><div hidden data-form-reasons><p data-reason="value_fails_source_integrity_check" data-title="Held">The value could not be reconciled with its own paper, so it is shown but not compared.</p><p data-reason="bounds_span_categories" data-title="Non-detect spans the bands">The result is below a reporting limit, and the range from zero to that limit crosses more than one band.</p><p data-reason="species_or_material_without_compatible_reference_after_full_ledger" data-title="No reference for this form">No medians or sample values exist for this form and metal, so no HMI reference could be built.</p><p data-reason="reference_below_display_minimum_after_full_ledger" data-title="Reference too small">The HMI reference for this form has fewer than four units or no measurable spread, below the display minimum.</p><p data-reason="bounded" data-title="Limits overlap">Reporting limits overlap the study comparison, so no direction can be shown.</p></div><script type="application/octet-stream" data-explorer-payload <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach nectarine

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared nectarine from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.

The HMI reference for lead in nectarine, juice pools 9 subgroup units from 1 sampling family, with a median of below 2 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 1.5 (lower quartile) and between 1.2 and 5 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health <a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for nectarine

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing nectarine for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 1 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 10 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

No maximum level for metals in nectarine was verified from primary text for this page; buyers should confirm the limit that applies in the destination market.

## Exposure from nectarine

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>

No source in the library converted nectarine concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.

Every source record that names nectarine was decided on its content: 2 occurrence extracted, 2 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Nectarine FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-nectarine" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-nectarine" data-question="What heavy metals are found in nectarine?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in nectarine?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, Cu, Fe, Mn and Zn in nectarine, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Mohammad Rezaei, Yousef Fakhri, Maryam Abtahi, Majid Keramati, Gholamreza Ghasemi, Mohammad H. Moradi, Hamed Shariatifar, Mahsa Mousavi Khaneghah · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Essential elements in the different type of fruits, soil and water samples collected from Markazi province, Iran: a health risk assessment study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Quality Assurance and Safety of Crops &amp; Foods</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/rezaei2020-fruits-markazi-essential-elements">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in nectarine, juice from 194 results in 3 reporting regions, with a subgroup median of 2.53 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-nectarine-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-nectarine-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in nectarine is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in nectarine is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 1 result from 1 source, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported inorganic arsenic in nectarine, juice from 3 results in 1 reporting region, with subgroup medians below 20 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
